# Changelog

What shipped, newest first. One line each; the web UI and server are live at https://electrophone.cc. Everything listed here is deployed except the sections marked "built, not deployed" (the 2026-10-14 release went out once the SSH access in [task-0027](tasks/task-0027.md) was restored; the live visualizer files match `origin/main` as of 2026-10-15). Why: [tech/decisions.md](tech/decisions.md); runbook: [tech/deploy.md](tech/deploy.md).

## 2026-10-27 (Release: server and web deployed as `5305ff0`)

- Deployed to https://electrophone.cc after a green `scripts/check.sh` (web 272 files, scripts, rule IDs, tokens, server 4937 passed): everything marked "to deploy" below, that is the artist fallback for YouTube album commits, the track number kept out of the artist in time-coded lists (ADD-199), album "Use for songs" (`POST /albums/{id}/cover/to-songs`), the album Type select height, the Library Songs, Albums and Drafts switch without a rebuild (LIB-263), the new web bundle hash, and the task-0122 server work (F4 module layering, F5 migration registry, F7 credits cleanup, F10 `error` key on plain refusals, F14 `backgroundjob` base; the F2 corrupt-file protection was already live).
- Docs and repo (task-0122 F13): the tracked dev files `edited.json`, `server/genre_eras.json` and `server/backups-*/applied.json` are gone and the `docs/ui-ux` PNGs are WebP; production keeps its data in `/var/lib/audioplayer`, not in `server/`, so the pull touched nothing it uses.
- Check fix: `studio-nav.test.mjs` pinned `route()` without the `soft` parameter that the Library keepalive added; the pin now matches `route(soft = false)`.

## 2026-10-27 (iOS: Albums and Playlists know when the server is offline; in source, not in a build)

- iOS (IOS-119; offline check, owner decision JZ1; both schemes build, all unit tests pass, checked in the simulator against a mock server, not run on a device): the album and playlist screens used to keep offering "Download album" and "Download" when the server was gone, and a tap showed "Downloading 0%" with no message. Cause: only the catalog sync and the check told the app the server was unreachable. Now every API call reports it (an answer of any status means reachable, a network failure means unreachable), so Albums, Playlists and their pages show "Offline" on the rows, a disabled Download album with the note "Offline. Downloads need the server." and Retry, no download starts while offline, and a download that fails leaves a "Retry" on its row. Nothing publishes unless the state really changed. Playback, queue and background downloads are unchanged.

## 2026-10-27 (Docs: UI/UX images compressed)

- Docs (task-0122 F13): the 38 design PNGs in `docs/ui-ux` (`alt-themes`, `mockups1`, `mockups2`, `vega-108`) are now WebP of the same pixel size, quality 85; 43 MB became 4.2 MB. Same file names with the `.webp` extension; the old PNGs are removed from the working tree only, git history is not rewritten, so the repository pack is unchanged.

## 2026-10-27 (iOS: Library play glyph and a shorter pinned header; in source, not in a build)

- iOS (IOS-117, IOS-118; UI/UX audit L4 and L7; both schemes build, all unit tests pass, checked in the iPhone SE simulator in light, dark and accessibility2, not run on a device): songs on the phone show a small play badge on their cover so the cover's job is visible (a row tap still opens Song Details), with a pressed state, and the glyph on the cover is now light in dark mode too. The Library search field and filter chips scroll away with the list, so the pinned header on an iPhone SE drops from about 280 pt to about 160 pt (title, Songs/Albums switch and controls stay pinned); the collapse-on-scroll logic is removed. Library v5 spec updated.

## 2026-10-27 (Web: Library Songs and Albums switch without a rebuild; deployed)

- Web (LIB-263; to deploy with the web bundle): clicking Songs, Albums or Drafts in the sidebar no longer rebuilds the whole Library page. Cause (measured on 800 songs and 40 albums): every click tore the page down, built all song rows again (twice, because the library refresh, the listening stats and the references each re-rendered it) and re-fetched `/library` and `/albums`, 1.2 to 6.7 s on a loaded machine. Now the page stays mounted and only the visible pane changes (3 to 75 ms), hidden song rows are rebuilt once at idle, an unchanged background library refresh does not re-render, a mounted Albums pane refreshes in place when older than 15 s, and hovering the Albums or Drafts item loads its module early

## 2026-10-27 (iOS: UI jitter while downloading and playing; in source, not in a build)

- iOS (IOS-116; both schemes build, all unit tests pass, measured in the simulator with a 1500-song library, not run on a device): the screen no longer stutters while songs download or play. Causes found: every download tick rebuilt the Library list (filter and sort of the whole library twice), the hidden Settings tab and the root view; the playback position checkpoint (every 5 s) rewrote UserDefaults and `@AppStorage` re-rendered the always-alive Statistics chart each time. Fixes: the percent of a download lives in its own `TransferProgress` and reaches only the rows that show it, throttled by `ProgressGate`; the Library results are computed once per change; Statistics and Albums use `@StoredValue`; redundant `ServerLink` publishes are skipped. Debug build, simulator, two downloads at a time: 3 to 54 frames per second while downloading, longest stall 1.7 s to 0.22 s, stalls every 5 s while playing gone. The recent PlayerService, NowPlaying and QueueAdvance refactor (IOS-115) added no publishes; the 0.5 s position clock was already separate.

## 2026-10-27 (Web: album popups and infobox; deployed, the "to deploy" notes below went live with the release above)

- Server (ADD-199; to deploy): Add album from a video no longer puts the track number into the artist when the list is written `01 Artist - Title 0:00` (a number without a dot, before or after the time); the number becomes the track number, and digit-led names such as `50 Cent` or `808 State` are left alone.
- Server and web: the album Edit dialog has a "Use for songs" button that copies the album's own cover to every linked song in one request (`POST /albums/{id}/cover/to-songs`); songs whose cover the user chose are kept until a second click confirms replacing them, and it reports "Set art on N songs" (LIB-262, API-305, task-0120; to deploy; iOS not built).
- Server and web: "Suggest links" button in the Links panel of Add album (both flows) and Edit album finds a Discogs master (else release) and an English Wikipedia album article for the artist and title and fills only the empty link fields (LIB-260, API-304, task-0120; to deploy).
- Server and web: Add album from a YouTube playlist now uses the video popup (shared form, Discogs auto-match and cover, one-line track labels with pencil, suggested genres, links from the description); the Discogs picker and track mapping are gone from the web (LIB-259, API-302, API-303, task-0120; to deploy).
- Web: the v1 Genres screen is removed and "Genres map v2" is now simply the Genres map at `#/genres` (`#/genres2` redirects); one sidebar item, one title; v1 outline, radial view, legend and toolbar code and CSS deleted, the v1 vocabulary stays as the tag vocabulary (GEN-101, WEB-084, task-0096; git tag `genres-map-v1`).

- Server and web: genres v2 keeps the curated genres untouched and adds a grey Legacy circle (collapsed until clicked) holding a complete copy of the v1 vocabulary, duplicates included (550 copies flagged `legacy: true`); the Genres map draws every curated circle at once as before (GEN-102, GEN-114, DATA-197, API-226, ADR-0045, task-0096; to deploy).
- Server: adding an album from a YouTube playlist fills the songs (Album, year, vocabulary genres, artist; only empty fields of existing songs and of songs linked to a track), the preview guesses artist, album, year and type from the title, and suggests genres (LIB-256, CAT-061, API-298, API-299, task-0120; to deploy).
- Web: Add album and Edit album popups reworked (no accidental close, token fields for title and artists, narrow year, Type select, one Genres field pre-filled from the preview, song-form Links panel, two-line track rows; no Advanced section, shift, last-end field or fade checkbox, the last track ends at the video duration) and the album infobox shows the linked song in a collapsed panel under each track (accordion, as before) with Link and Unlink icons, redrawn from Alex's two drawn icons (`docs/brand/icons/link.svg`, `unlink.svg`; the same two icons are used by the Open source link on the player); tracks without a song show an azure Link icon on the row itself and the sub-row text sits closer to the Unlink icon (LIB-253 to LIB-255, task-0120; to deploy, focused tests only).
- Web: Add album track rows are read-only text labels ("1. Artist - Title [Version] (8:49)") with a pencil icon that opens the start time, Artist, Title and Version fields on demand; the "Artist is the channel" note is gone (LIB-258, task-0120; to deploy, focused tests only).
- Web: Add album from a single video has "Paste a track list with lengths" (lines like "1 Title 9:18", optional Artist - Title, Total length line ignored): starts are the running sum of the lengths, a muted "Track list is N:NN shorter/longer than the video" line offers "Scale to video length" (LIB-261, task-0120; to deploy, focused tests only).
- Server and web: Add album from a single YouTube video uses the same title, artist, year, type and genre guess as the playlist flow ("Artist - Album (2025) [FULL ALBUM]" is an Album, not a Mix), reads Discogs, Wikipedia and RateYourMusic links from the description and prefills from the Discogs release, and has a "Find on Discogs" button (`POST /albums/discogs/search`) (API-300, API-301, LIB-257, task-0120; to deploy, focused tests only).
- Server and web: Add album from a YouTube video without a Discogs link in the description searches Discogs masters for the guessed artist and album and applies the one exact match (link, year, genres, type, cover; "Matched on Discogs" line), otherwise offers the candidates; master links work like release links; the cover is the Discogs image (also for Find on Discogs picks), the thumbnail only as a fallback, and the Add dialog shows a cover preview (API-300, LIB-252, task-0120; to deploy, focused tests only).

Two drifts fixed: Swift `decadeTag` stopped at 2029 (it now follows the current year plus one like the web and the server), and the macOS force simulation split the link pull by the source degree instead of the target degree.

## 2026-10-26 (Reliability phase of the architecture audit; server deployed 2026-10-27, iOS in build 20261035)

- Server (task-0122 F2, DATA-208, API-296; `test_userjson` and the neighbouring suites pass): a corrupt `drafts.json`, `playlists.json`, `albums.json` or `genre_layout.json` is no longer read as empty and then overwritten by the next save; it is copied to `<name>.corrupt-<stamp>`, logged, listed in the owner `/health` as `corrupt_files`, and its routes answer 500 `data_corrupt` without writing. The server still starts.
- iOS and tests (task-0122 F3, IOS-108; both schemes build, `AudioPlayerTests` pass, web and server parity tests pass): shared case files `url_clean_cases.json` and `tag_cases.json` join `token_cases.json` in `server/tests/fixtures` and are checked by the server, the web and, as resources of `AudioPlayerTests`, the Swift ports of tokens, url-clean and tags. The first run found the Swift tokenizer still split on commas, semicolons, periods and bullets and on an unspaced `|`, against ADD-071 and the web; `Tokens.swift` now follows the web rules (iOS Add form and song sheet only, not run on a device). The two `#if DEBUG` self-checks became XCTest.
- iOS (task-0122 F1, IOS-107; `LibraryIndexTests` pass, both schemes build, not run on a device or in airplane mode): the on-phone state no longer lives only in the catalog cache. `phone-index.json` holds the file map and the records of the songs on the phone; a `library.json` that fails to decode is copied aside, the last good copy or the phone index stands in, one bad song record no longer drops the file, and a lost map is rebuilt from file names. Needs a TestFlight build and a device check before it counts as done.

## 2026-10-26 (Album from a time-coded YouTube video; server and web deployed)

- Server and web (task-0038 phase 1B, API-264 to API-270, API-290 to API-295; `test_albums_video`, `test_video_split`, `test_album_video_flow`, `album-video` and `album-video-model` tests pass): Add album accepts a single YouTube video link, reads the track list from the chapters, the description or a pasted text, shows an editable preview table (times, artists, titles, versions, "In library" rows with Use existing, shift all times, fades), and creates the album with one metadata-only song per ticked track; a split job downloads the audio once, cuts one file per track with ffmpeg and marks each song's audio as on the server; the whole split counts as one add. The web shows the job progress and opens the album when it is done. Create errors (already exists, album limit, wrong times) show readable messages. Live on https://electrophone.cc; ffmpeg is installed on perkunas, but a real cut has not been run yet (the tests fake it), and the iOS side is not built.

- Web (WEB-212, WEB-213, STU-058, STU-059, task-0114; owner decision IN1: each harmonic is its own sine wave and the sound is their sum, so show them; no tests run, `node --check`, the builds, node numeric checks and the Browser pane only, test files written and edited, not run): a "Harmonics stack" block with one sine row per harmonic (first 4, 8 or 12, labelled with the real Hz and percent), a bottom Sum row, Build up on Primitive, an Animate checkbox (one frame loop, off by default) and a hidden table for screen readers. Primitive: under the one-cycle scope for every waveform. Chordophones: rows follow the harmonics count, roll-off, Mute H1, inharmonicity and strike position. New Glossary entry "Harmonic decomposition" (111 terms, 168 relations). The Sum of 8 square rows matches the 32-harmonic scope partial sum within 0.09 % of the peak. Not verified: Safari, a phone, a screen reader.

## 2026-10-25 (Sound wave animation: free-field cone motion, deployed to studio.electrophone.cc as `fc2fbb5`)

- Web (STU-043, STU-047, STU-055, STU-044; owner decision 2026-10-25, "do": the cone moves like a real speaker; no tests run, test assertions edited, `node --check`, the build, node numeric checks and the Browser pane with a stepped clock): the cone position is now the double integral of the pressure shape (`coneTable`, `coneShape`, second zero-mean `Float32Array` table, peak 1), so the pressure follows the cone's acceleration instead of its speed. Sine: anti-phase with the pressure; square: smooth parabolic arcs, no straight lines; triangle: cubic-like; sawtooth: one gentle kink. Dots, density curve and colours unchanged. Captions, the Learn page "How to read this" bullet and the Sound wave Glossary text are rewritten, a "Simplified model" note sits under the animation, and Features lists the change.

## 2026-10-25 (Primitive custom waveform on the studio site, deployed to studio.electrophone.cc as `7e244c2`)

- Web (WEB-211, STU-057, task-0114; owner decision IM2: allow a custom waveform on Primitive defined by harmonics sliders, not a freehand draw; no tests run, `node --check`, the builds, node numeric checks and the Browser pane only, test files written and edited, not run): the Waveform switch has a fifth option, Custom (also `#/synthesize/simple?wave=custom`). It turns the harmonics chart into an editor of up to 32 signed harmonic bars (-100 to +100 percent of H1, drag with the pointer, Shift for 0.1 steps, or arrow keys, Home, End and Delete on each bar, which is a `role="slider"`), with hover and focus frequencies ("H3 · 1320 Hz"), the presets Sine, Triangle, Square, Sawtooth, Organ, Hollow, Only overtones (the missing fundamental), Clear and Reset, a "Show each harmonic" option, and a "Build up: show the first N harmonics" slider (with "Play the build-up too"). The sound is a `PeriodicWave` of the bars with the same RMS loudness compensation and 80 percent cap as the other shapes, updated at most once per frame without a click. Glossary: Fourier series and Additive synthesis (110 terms, 166 relations); Features: "Primitive: custom waveform from harmonics". Measured: the 32-harmonic square top is flat within 4.6 percent; 100 ms rendered offline at 440 Hz and 30 percent amplitude gives RMS 0.2121 for the sine, the custom sine, square, triangle, sawtooth, Organ, Hollow and Only overtones, against 0.2110 for the native square (0.5 percent), peaks at most 0.557; at 375 px the 16 bars fit and the 32 bars scroll inside the chart. Not verified: how it sounds, a real finger, Safari.

## 2026-10-25 (Studio: Clicks to tone, deployed to studio.electrophone.cc as `f7932d7`; the main app bundle is unchanged)

- Web (STU-048, STU-005, STU-006, STU-026, task-0114; owner decision IC1: rhythm and pitch are the same repetition at different speeds; no tests run, `node --check`, the builds, node checks, an `OfflineAudioContext` render and the Browser pane): new Synthesize page "Clicks to tone" after Primitive (`#/synthesize/clicks`, alias `#/clicks`, `web/js/clicks-tone.js`): a click rate from 1 to 1000 per second (number and logarithmic slider, default 2) with the readout "N clicks per second = B BPM = N Hz", a regime label (rhythm, fast pulses, the border at 15 to 30 per second, tone with note name and cents above 30), six preset chips (120 BPM, 600 BPM, 1000 BPM Moby Thousand, 1200 BPM, 100 Hz, A4 440 Hz), Amplitude (whole percents, default 30, cap 80), Length or Continuous and Play / Stop with "i" popup hints. Sound: from 25 per second an `OscillatorNode` with an equal-coefficient `PeriodicWave` (an ideal band-limited impulse train, peak exactly the amplitude, sample-accurate at any rate, smooth rate changes); below 25 per second a 100 ms lookahead scheduler of 2 ms noise-burst `AudioBufferSourceNode`s (an impulse train there has almost no energy and is a faint tick), the two cross-fading with a hysteresis; no AudioWorklet, CSP unchanged. Views: "Waveform: clicks over time" (the shared `drawScope`, a window of about 8 periods between 5 ms and 4 s, caption "8 clicks in 4 s") and the 16 equal harmonics bars (titled "Frequencies in the rhythm (infrasound: below hearing)" below 20 per second). Glossary: Click train, Flutter and roughness, Repetition rate (108 terms, 163 relations; Physics of sound 25, Perception 6). Checked: a one-second render at 2 per second has 2 peaks and at 440 per second 440, every peak within 0.5 samples of the ideal position and at most 0.3 at 30 percent; the map layouts still have no overlaps. The sound itself was not heard. Tests edited and added (`clicks-tone.test.mjs`, `studio-site.test.mjs`, `glossary-map.test.mjs`, `glossary-terms.test.mjs`), not run.

## 2026-10-25 (Studio Rhythm: lane BPM reads the gaps between hits and gets a "Lane BPM window" switch, deployed to studio.electrophone.cc and electrophone.cc as `cb615a1`)

- Web (STU-042, WEB-191, task-0073, task-0108; owner report: with 12 bands on real music every lane read "BPM -" although five lanes showed regular ticks about 0.42 s apart; no tests run, `node --check`, the build, node runs of the estimator on synthetic ticks and a Browser pane check with a fake 120 BPM microphone only): on a synthetic train like the screenshot (period 0.42 s, 85 percent of ticks present, extra ticks between, 20 to 50 ms jitter) the crest rule passed (crest 50 against a minimum of 6) and the confidence floor of 0.5 rejected it (autocorrelation peak 0.32 to 0.51; at 50 ms jitter the lock rule of 0.3 rejected it first), with 2P or 3P winning the octave tie (72 or 48 BPM). The lane estimate is now `estimateIntervals` in `live-bpm.js`: a histogram of the pair gaps of 0.2 to 2 s, the smallest well-explained period wins, accepted with at least 4 periods in the window, 60 percent of ticks explained, confidence 0.4 and 3 standard deviations over random ticks; tolerance the larger of 8 percent and 40 ms. Node, 60 trains: before 21 of 60 read about 143 at 20 ms jitter and 0 of 60 at 50 ms; after, in the 4 s window 48 of 60 and 17 of 60 (all read about 143 BPM, confidence 0.84), in the 8 s window 58 of 60 and 23 of 60. Random Poisson ticks at 2.4 per second: a dash in 197 to 200 of 200 trials in every window. A 120 BPM pattern reads kick 120.0, snare 60.0 `x1/2` (from the 8 s window; 4 s holds only 4 periods), hats 240 `x2`, as before. All 12 lanes cost at most 0.6 ms per second (30 s window). A segmented "Lane BPM window" switch (1 s, 4 s, 8 s, 15 s, 30 s; default 4 s) sits next to "Bands" on Rhythm and the Chordophones lab; a lane shows a dash while it has less than a window of history or fewer than 4 periods of its tempo, so lane BPMs appear after about 4 s. Browser pane: values at 5 s, the switch re-reads at once, 375 px no horizontal scroll, no console errors. Not checked with a real microphone or a real track.

## 2026-10-25 (Studio Rhythm: the 60 s window stays a dash on real music, fixed, deployed to studio.electrophone.cc and electrophone.cc as `d2c0fa8`)

- Web (WEB-208, STU-041, task-0073; owner bug report; no tests run, `node --check` and node checks of the engine on synthetic audio with 4096-sample blocks at 48 000 and 44 100 Hz only; not seen with a real microphone or a real track): the 60 s readout stayed a dash while the other windows filled. Cause: not the history length (the buffer holds the 60 s window at every sample rate, the readout fills at 61 s on a steady kick and hat) but the disagreement filter. On a kick, snare and hat pattern the long windows read the two-beat period (128 BPM track: 64 in the 8 to 60 s windows, mostly), and a reading more than 30 percent slower than the shorter windows was dropped, so the 60 s window, the longest and the most likely to find that period, was almost always empty. Now a long window that reads exactly half of the nearest shorter reading (within 3 percent) is shown doubled, in the octave of the shorter window; two-tempo readings (30 across 120 and 150) and every other mismatch are still dropped. Synthetic 128 BPM track with variation and drift, readouts of the 60 s window at 61, 70, 80 and 89 s: dash, dash, dash, dash before; 128, 128, 128, 128 after. Steady 120 BPM kick and hat: 15, 30, 45 s dash (not full), 61 and 70 s 120.0 before and after. The Browser pane does not run `requestAnimationFrame` for a page it does not display, so the full in-page 75 s run could not be made there.

- Web (STU-025, STU-037, STU-038, task-0114, new [task-0118](tasks/task-0118.md); owner decisions HQ3 and HR1; no tests run, `node --check`, the build, node checks of the data (ids and aliases unique, every link endpoint exists, no overlapping pills or boxes in both layouts) and the Browser pane only): 19 new Glossary terms. Waveforms and noise (Physics of sound): Sawtooth wave (saw; all harmonics, 1/n), Square wave (meander, меандр; odd harmonics, 1/n), Triangle wave (odd harmonics, 1/n squared), Pulse wave, Duty cycle, White noise, Pink noise (3 dB per octave down). Analysis tools: Fourier transform (FFT) and Spectrum analyzer (spectrometer, spectral analyzer; "Spectrum" stays its own entry). Rhythm (Music): Rhythm, Beat, Tempo (now its own entry, no longer an alias of BPM), Meter (time signature), Bar (measure), Downbeat, Onset, Syncopation, Subdivision, Swing. Loudness gets the alias "volume" and the volume-versus-power explanation (doubling the amplitude is 4x the power, +6 dB, but only somewhat louder; about +10 dB sounds twice as loud; the ear hears mid tones better than deep bass), and Power points to it. 30 new links (the Map now has 73 terms and 106 links); the boxes stay balanced (wide area: Physics 369 x 410 in two columns, Music 559 x 373 in three; tall area: at most two columns, Music 383 x 478, so the phone view still opens at about 70 percent; no overlaps). There is no Waveforms page: it is the ticket [task-0118](tasks/task-0118.md) with open questions and a recommendation.

The view moves elements in place (a pan is one `transform`; a zoom updates coordinates), no scaled group. Pure helpers `pillScale` and `placeLabels` in `web/js/glossary-map.js`, tests added and not run.

## 2026-10-25 (Primitive air dots follow the oscilloscope pressure, with a density curve and the cone a quarter cycle behind, deployed to studio.electrophone.cc and the main app as `0894179`)

- Web (WEB-204, WEB-201; owner decision HM, "waves should correspond, no?"; no tests run, `node --check`, the build and the Browser pane with a virtual clock): the air dots now show the pressure, which follows the cone's velocity, not its displacement: the density at distance x equals the oscilloscope pressure at t - x/c, so the densest bunch is centred on the cone when the oscilloscope dot is on its peak and the dots at the cone are furthest apart at the trough (measured spacing 0.832 and 1.168 of the rest gap at 30 % amplitude). A thin "air density" curve above the dot rows draws the oscilloscope curve mirrored in time ("now" at the cone, "one cycle ago" at the right edge) with a marker at the cone (matches the scope curve within 0.05 % of the peak at five positions). The cone is drawn a quarter cycle behind the pressure (offset -cos(phase)) with the caption sentence "The cone is a quarter cycle behind the pressure: ..."; the oscilloscope subtitle and the captions say the dot is the pressure now. The cone canvas is 184 px tall. `simple-tone.test.mjs` updated, not run.

## 2026-10-25 (Studio Trail length acts on the existing ghost at once, deployed to studio.electrophone.cc as `3723245`; the main app is unchanged, its bundle did not change)

- Web (WEB-205, STU-028; owner: "trail length is not live"; no tests run, `node --check`, the build and the Browser pane with a faked analyser only): each bin now stores its last peak and the time of the peak, and the ghost is `peak - rate x (now - t_peak)` with the current rate every frame (`trailLevel`, `holdTrail`), so moving the counter reshapes the ghost already on screen in the same frame instead of changing only its future fall. Cause: the old buffer fell incrementally, so a new length only changed the later fall (the counter was present on the live site and reached the draw loop). 440 Hz burst, 5 s to 1 s by minus clicks or typing, ghost 1 s later: -70.8 dB before the fix (-30.5 right after the change), -97.2 dB after (-56.9 right after); one frame is no longer capped at 250 ms, a long pause now fades the ghost by real time. `listen-views.test.mjs` adjusted, not run.

## 2026-10-25 (Studio Spectrum octave-error correction, deployed to studio.electrophone.cc as `514a082`; the main app is not redeployed, its bundle did not change)

- Web (WEB-206, STU-029, task-0107; owner screenshot: peaks at 84, 166, 250, 330, 415, 495, 577 Hz with the 166 Hz one loudest, H1 drawn on 166 Hz and the peaks at 84, 250 and 415 Hz shown as missing; no tests run, a node run on synthetic signals and the Browser pane only): "Mark harmonics" now checks for an octave error. After the YIN estimate, `correctOctave` tries f0/4, f0/3 and f0/2 on the spectrum and takes the lowest candidate whose H1 is a peak at least 25 dB above the floor and whose harmonics (including the ones between those of f0) are found; the tracker holds an octave flip until 3 estimates agree. The readout adds "; lower than the loudest peak (166 Hz)". Node (48 kHz, YIN alone, then with the correction): the owner-like tone (H1 -42, H2 -26, H3 -45, H4 -52 ... dBFS at 83.9 Hz) 168.1 to 84.1 Hz (also with noise at -60 and -50 dBFS and at 44.1 kHz); sine 220, sawtooth 220, odd-only 220, H2 to H8 of 220, voice-like 150 Hz, the C4 E4 G4 and C3 E3 G3 chords, the 60 Hz and 2 kHz edges and white noise are unchanged. Glossary "Octave error". New `pitch-detect.test.mjs` and `listen-views.test.mjs` cases, not run.

## 2026-10-24 (harmonics lab: piano realism, web, deployed as `31f8b87`)

- Web (WEB-189; owner request 2026-10-24; no tests run, `node --check` and a local Chromium page at 375 px only): under "Strike position" a collapsed "Why the strike position matters" explains that the hammer's node harmonic nearly vanishes, why makers use about 1/7 to 1/9, and to try 1/10; the fractions follow the selected position.
- Web (task-0109, WEB-189, ADR-0048 amended; owner decision GB3, 2026-10-24: "still not like a real piano"; no tests run, `node --check` and a local Chromium page with the real `AudioContext` and node-creation hooks only): the "Pitch and harmonics" card has a "Piano realism" group, shown while "Piano-like decay" is on, four switches on by default: "Hammer thump" (a short filtered noise burst at each strike, level -48 to -12 dB relative to H1, default -24), "Two-stage decay" (a prompt part plus a slower aftersound, 0.75 and 0.25 at 1 and 4 times the fade time), "Inharmonicity" (partial n at `n f sqrt(1 + B n^2)`, B 0 to 0.002, default 0.0004; labels show the real Hz) and "Hammer position" (1/12 to 1/4 of the string, default 1/8; each partial times `|sin(n pi p)|`, floor 0.03, so H8 is quiet at 1/8). Every change applies at the next strike; with the mode off the sound is unchanged. Checked at 440 Hz: H10 at 4487.14 Hz, H8 about 25 dB down, the thump 15 to 30 dB above the between-partial floor, a longer tail, B moving only at the strike, no horizontal scroll at 375 px. Not verified: how it sounds, and the Mac app.

## 2026-10-24 (harmonics lab: piano-like decay mode, web, deployed as `7e3055a`)

- Web (task-0109, WEB-184, WEB-185, ADR-0048; owner decision FW2, 2026-10-24: the steady tone "sounds like a chainsaw, never like a piano"; no tests run, `node --check` and a local Chromium page with the real `AudioContext` only): the "Pitch and harmonics" card has a "Piano-like decay" checkbox (off by default, off is the steady tone). On, the tone strikes by itself every "Repeat every" seconds (1 to 6, default 3): a 5 ms attack, then each harmonic fades exponentially with `tau_n = tau1 / n^0.8` ("Fade time (H1)" 0.2 to 5 s, default 1.2), so the high ones die first; a re-strike ramps to zero over 4 ms first, and everything is scheduled on the `AudioParam` timeline (a 50 ms timer only enqueues strikes 0.2 s ahead). The views show the real signal. Measured: H1, H3, H6 and H10 gains and analyser levels fell at different rates (in 0.4 s the H10 level fell about 16 dB, H1 about 1.3 dB) and jumped back at each strike about 3 s apart. Defaults are now 10 harmonics and -12 dB/oct. Code: `web/js/harmonics.js`, `web/studio.css`. Not verified: how it sounds, and the Mac app.

## 2026-10-24 (live BPM analyzer fixed, web, deployed)

- Web (task-0073, LIB-019a; deployed 2026-10-24 as `69c4c00`; owner report 2026-10-24: "~127" on a 1000 BPM track and the same value for other songs; no tests run, throwaway node scripts and a local server with synthetic fixtures only): the badge never left `~<stored>` because the analyser it reads exists only after the visualizer, Fade or loop built the audio graph, so on a fresh page the detector got no samples for any song; the toggle now builds the graph. The detector is rewritten: PCM from the analyser time-domain buffer, a 400 Hz onset envelope, autocorrelation over 6 s, range 30 to 1200 BPM (the old 60 to 200 window with a 120 prior folded 1000 BPM to about 127), the shortest lag within 5% of the strongest peak wins, the stored BPM and the usual range break only near ties (15%), median of the last 3 estimates, steady noise or tones give no value. Synthetic click and kick tracks: 30 to 1200 BPM exact within 0.1% clean and within 2% in 297 of 300 jitter and noise variants; old against new on 600 random 60 to 200 BPM variants (phase, jitter up to 6 ms, noise): 0 regressions, new right in 599, old in 517, largest difference 0.43 BPM; local browser readings through the real card: 120/140/174 and a 120-140-174 step file followed within 6 s, 1000 BPM click track shows `1000` after 5 s; about 3 to 8 ms per one-second analysis. ## 2026-10-24 (Studio Tools tab with a microphone BPM meter, web, deployed)

- Web (deployed 2026-10-24 as `5b2684a`; owner request 2026-10-24, "additionally create another tab in studio - tools. temporary" and "place there live bpm analyzer, so on my phone I can activate it to listen to BPM from the microphone"; WEB-175 to WEB-179, task-0072; `node --check`, a local Chromium with a fake microphone only, no tests run): the Studio has two tabs, Track (as before, `#/studio`) and Tools (`#/studio/tools`, works without a song, deep link and Back work, the last tab is not remembered). The first tool, "Live BPM (microphone)", listens through `getUserMedia` with echo cancellation, noise suppression and auto gain off, shows a large BPM badge ("- - - BPM" when idle or silent), a level meter and a state line, and uses the same analyser engine as the player's live BPM (`mic-bpm.js` adapter). Not verified: a real microphone on a phone. The macOS app has no microphone entitlement, so the tool is for the browser only (see task-0072).

## 2026-10-24 (SonarCloud vulnerability review: 4 hardened, 9 false positives, web and server, deployed)

- Server, web and build script (owner decision FG1, 2026-10-24; deployed 2026-10-24 as `333d77d`; WEB-138, ADD-006; [review table](tech/sonar-baseline.md#vulnerability-review-2026-10-24); no tests run, existing assertions unaffected, each fix exercised against a local server with a throwaway data directory): `server/app/features.py` ignores recorded deploys whose hash is not 7 to 40 hex characters or whose date is not `YYYY-MM-DD` and refuses any `git log` revision that is not `HEAD`, a hash or a hash range (a stored `--output=<file>` hash used to make git write that file); `server/app/extractor.py` `fetch_cover` requests only `https` port 443 without credentials to `i.ytimg.com/vi/<11-character id>/hqdefault.jpg` or an `sndcdn.com` host; `web/js/play-recorder.js` builds its fallback batch key from `crypto.getRandomValues` instead of `Math.random` (`web/dist/` rebuilt); `scripts/build-web.mjs` starts npm through the node binary instead of a `PATH` lookup. The other nine findings (id-validated cover paths, constant state paths, owner-only CLI arguments) are false positives, listed for the owner to mark in the SonarCloud UI.

## 2026-10-24 (Lighthouse wins 1 and 4: pre-built web bundle and one stylesheet, web and server, deployed)

- Web and server (owner decision FF2, 2026-10-24; deployed 2026-10-24 as `cc70521`; [ADR-0047](tech/decisions.md), WEB-171, API-051; `node --check`, a local server and a browser check only, no tests run): the committed `web/dist/` (made by `node scripts/build-web.mjs`, esbuild 0.27.7) replaces 14 stylesheets with one `app.css` and 131 modules with `app.js`, `icons.js`, 6 shared startup chunks and two lazy groups (dialogs, pages and visualizer; both Genres maps); the server uses it only while its manifest hash equals the current sources and `AUDIO_WEB_BUNDLE` is not `0`, otherwise it serves the unbundled files as before, so editing `web/js` and reloading needs no build. `scripts/deploy.sh` stops when `web/dist` is stale ([deploy.md](tech/deploy.md#web-bundle)). Local desktop Lighthouse against the unbundled and the bundled server behind the same gzip proxy (median of 3): requests 126 to 25, first contentful paint 737 ms to 501 ms, largest contentful paint 2749 ms to 642 ms, performance 0.85 to 1.00; computed styles of every element equal on 12 views at 1280 px and 2 at 390 px. Live (https://electrophone.cc, same Lighthouse, median of 3): requests 174 to 30, first contentful paint 1109 ms to 546 ms, largest contentful paint 2365 ms to 689 ms, performance 0.85 to 1.00; the bundle files are `immutable` and `cf-cache-status: HIT` from the second request, and the console shows only the three 401s of the signed-out page.

## 2026-10-24 (Lighthouse quick wins: smaller placeholder, immutable icons, cacheable page shell, stable sign-in, web and server, deployed)

## 2026-10-24 (a YouTube playlist added as drafts makes a playlist, deployed)

- Server and web (owner request 2026-10-24: "currently when I add a draft with a YouTube playlist, it creates separate entries... can you also create a playlist and add these entries in it?"; [task-0059](tasks/task-0059.md) Drafts variant, decisions A to D confirmed with the recommended answers; LIB-214 to LIB-218, ADD-192, PLS-135 to PLS-138, API-239, API-240, DATA-200, DATA-201; deployed 2026-10-24 as `80556b6`; no tests run or written, test assertions edited in `web/js/drafts-import.test.mjs`, `web/js/drafts-import-ui.test.mjs` and `server/tests/test_drafts.py`; checked by hand against a local server with a stubbed playlist and lookups): importing a YouTube playlist into the Drafts now stores on each draft where it came from (`playlist: {id, title, position}`, additive, old drafts untouched), and "Add to Library" on such a draft calls `POST /drafts/{id}/playlist` once after the save. The server creates the user's playlist named after the YouTube title, or extends the one linked to that YouTube playlist by id (`source` on the playlist, internal), inserting songs by their YouTube position so several batches in any order give the YouTube order; a different playlist with the same name gives "Name (2)". Entries already in the Library are kept as drafts marked "In library" (no lookup) and are swept into the playlist with the next saved sibling, or by their own "Add to playlist" button; swept drafts leave the list. The toast reads "Added N songs to playlist <name>" with an Open link, the Drafts row says "from <title>", and a cap hit (200 playlists, 2000 songs, non-owners) leaves the song in the Library and the draft in place. By hand: 8 entries, 2 of them in the Library (one by video id, one by title), saved in the order 6, 2, 7, 8, 4, 1 gave a playlist in order 1 to 8 (the first save added 3 songs, the in-Library siblings among them), a second playlist with the same title became "Chill Mix Test (2)" and took all 8 through "Add to playlist". Not covered: the Add dialog's own playlist import view and drafts that existed before.

After, both states have the mark centre at 32, brand block 8-56, toggle 64, first item 68, divider 328, Help 341, Settings 377, row top 585 and play centre 620, stamp box 658-684 (26px), measured with `getBoundingClientRect` in same-size iframes at 1200x800, 1000x700, 800x700 and 800x420, with and without a loaded song; the item boxes stay 32px high and nothing moves at any frame of the width transition. The rail now uses the open sidebar's top padding, the same 48px brand block and 30px mark, the same toggle offset (outside the menu column, no overlap with the first item) and the same `--side-lockup-gap`; the hidden stamp keeps its box in the rail, the menu margin above the player row applies in both states, and the rail's round play button is centred on the open row's play button.

## 2026-10-24 (zenonesque and suomisaundi added, nitzhonot description rewritten, deployed)

- Server data (owner request 2026-10-24; GEN-056, GEN-103, GEN-105, DATA-196, DATA-197, task-0096; deployed 2026-10-24 as `1e08011`, load checks only, no tests run, test assertions edited): `suomisaundi` (Rate Your Music: 175 releases, "a Finnish-based deviation of Psytrance") is an approved extra under `psytrance`, level 3, with `related` goa trance and synonyms `spugedelic trance`, `suomisoundi` and `suomistyge`; `zenonesque` (48 releases, "a form of Progressive Psytrance") is an approved extra under `progressive psytrance`, level 4, with `related` minimal techno and tech trance and synonyms `zenon` and `zenon sound`. ## 2026-10-24 (flat playlists, server side, deployed)

- Server (owner decision 2026-10-24, deployed 2026-10-24 as `67a7361`, [task-0058](tasks/task-0058.md) phase 1, ADR-0022 amended; PLS-110 to PLS-120, API-229 to API-238, DATA-199; no tests written by the owner's rule, checked by hand against a local server): per-user flat playlists in `users/<uid>/playlists.json`, each `{id (pl_ + 8 characters), name, description, created, updated, songs: [song ids]}`, private to the signed-in user (guests and anonymous get 401). Routes `GET/POST /playlists`, `GET/PATCH/DELETE /playlists/{id}`, `POST /playlists/{id}/songs` (duplicates and ids not in the library are skipped and reported), `DELETE /playlists/{id}/songs/{song_id}`, `PUT /playlists/{id}/order` (exact permutation, else 422 `bad_order`) and `GET /playlists/{id}/export`, a text file with one `Artists - Title (Version) - m:ss` line per song. Names are unique per user (409 `playlist_name_taken`); non-owners get 200 playlists and 2000 songs per playlist (422). Deleting a library song removes its id from the user's playlists; the sync's inbound check lists the file. No web page yet (the Playlists page is still the placeholder).

## 2026-10-24 (web playback end and reconnect fix, deployed)

- Web fix: a song that reached its end no longer sits at 7:42 / 7:42 with the pause icon after a server restart, and a lone song with Shuffle and Repeat whole list loops (PLY-200 to PLY-203, WEB-140; owner report 2026-10-23; deployed 2026-10-24, checked in the Chrome browser pane against a local API with an injected cut stream, Safari and a device not tried, no tests run, test assertions edited): a watchdog and a near-end rule (`web/js/end-guard.js`) run the normal end-of-song path when the browser never fires `ended` (stalled tail, media error in the last 3 s or 2 %); the pause icon follows the element (a failure that is not retried now pauses it); the media retry window is about 31 s instead of 5 s, remembers that the user wanted playback (Chrome pauses before `error`, so the reload used to stay silent on "Reconnecting...") and backs off when the stream breaks again at once; Shuffle + Repeat whole list on a single playable song restarts it instead of stopping with "No downloaded songs to shuffle" (`shufflePlayable` in `queue-logic.js`).

## 2026-10-21 (Wikipedia cover lookup fix, server and web, to deploy)

- Server: the Change cover dialog found no Wikipedia cover for single and album pages because MediaWiki `pageimages` leaves out non-free (fair use) infobox images (Birdy - Wings); `cover_candidates` now tries the REST page summary first (`thumbnail` / `originalimage`, 1024 px url), then `pageimages`, then the article's file list (`prop=images` and `imageinfo`, skipping logos, flags and icons), for any `*.wikipedia.org` link; Wikimedia API calls and image downloads send `Electrophone/1.0 (https://electrophone.cc)` as User-Agent and `utm_*` parameters are dropped from fetched image URLs (API-202, task-0054, to deploy; tests written, not run, never tried against live Wikipedia).
- Web: the Change cover dialog shows the preview frame only when an image or the default placeholder is selected, no empty dark square under "No Wikipedia covers found" (task-0054, to deploy; test written, not run).

## 2026-10-20 (the server listens first, startup work in the background, built, not deployed)

- Server and web (task-0071, CV1, API-188, WEB-140): the git recovery and the migrations no longer run before uvicorn accepts connections, so a deploy is no longer a long 502 window. The server listens at once and runs them in a background thread; until they are done every data route answers `503 {"detail":"starting"}` with `Retry-After: 3` while `/health` (now with `ready` and `startup`), the page and the static files are served; `AUDIO_STARTUP_MAX_S` (300) releases the gate, a failing migration is logged and the server still becomes ready. The web retries a starting server for about 20 s with a "Server is starting..." note, the sidebar LED turns amber, `scripts/deploy.sh` waits for `ready`. Measured on a copy of the 423-song library: first `/health` 200 after 1.0 to 2.0 s, ready after 1.4 to 2.3 s. Not deployed.

## 2026-10-20 (temporary Others node replaces Electronic, data and server, built, not deployed)

- Server and data (task-0066, T2): `app.migrate_retired` removes the retired genre `electronic` (`server/data/retired_genres.json`) from the songs of every user once at server start, a generic pass separate from the owner removals; songs whose only genre it was stay without one. Backup, report and marker in `backups-genre-retired/<uid>/`, one git commit per user, switch `AUDIO_GENRE_RETIRED=0` (TAG-111, [runbook](tech/deploy.md#genre-retired), tests `test_migrate_retired.py`). Dry run on a copy of the live library: 170 songs touched, 7 left without a genre. The owner one-off genre fixes (TAG-085) never add a retired or non-canonical genre, so a restart does not bring `electronic` back (startup idempotence test in `test_migrate_retired.py`). Not deployed.
- Data and server (task-0066, T1, owner decision 2026-10-20): the family `Electronic` is renamed to a temporary group `Others` (`group: true`, `temporary: true` in `genre_levels.json`): a level 1 node that is never a vocabulary entry and never assigned to a song; the 146 genres that were under Electronic (51 direct children, the rest below them, house > deep house, psytrance > goa trance unchanged) have Others as parent instead, levels unchanged. `electronic` is not a genre any more: not canonical (stored `genre:electronic` tags are kept and listed by the legacy-tag review, writes get 422, never suggested); its aliases `dance & edm`, `club`, `electronica`, `indietronica`, `alternative dance`, `rave` are dropped (`edm` and its aliases stay). `GET /genres/vocabulary` gains `groups` (Others: assignable false, temporary true, colour Electronic, members) and `forms` (promoted genres, empty now); `families` lists Others in the old Electronic slot; `flavours` and `subfamilies` stay. `/genres/graph` keeps the 11 legacy families (Others and every genre promoted out of it map to Electronic). New `scripts/genre-ops.py` (promote a genre to level 1, move or re-parent a genre, review before/after) so the next restructure steps are small data edits. Not deployed; the song-side removal of the `electronic` pill is T2, the web (Others node, groups) is T3.
- Web (task-0066, T3, GEN-083, WEB-114): the Genres map and the genre pickers read the new vocabulary: the top node is derived from `families` plus `groups` and `forms`, no family name is hardcoded any more. `Others` is a grey group node (neutral palette slot, dashed outline, no song count and no Library button, a "temporary group" badge and an infobox-free note "Others - a temporary group: organisational, never assigned to songs" when it is selected); the genres below it are shades of the neutral (subfamily ladder on the neutral base); the song card shows them on a line that starts with the Others chip. The genre field never offers or accepts a group, its old names or `electronic` (a hint says so). A stored `genre:electronic` that is still on a song is not counted anywhere and shows as typed on the line without a family. Any number of level 1 forms (promoted genres) render generically with their `display` label and the colour of their group (the legacy Electronic slot) until they get their own, with a spare-slot fallback. Not deployed.
- Data and server (task-0066 step 1, GEN-084, owner decision 2026-10-20, "move dnb, trance, synthwave, techno, house, synthpop, disco out of Others"): house, techno, trance, drum and bass and synthwave are level 1 genres now (`scripts/genre-ops.py promote`; the `forms` block of `GET /genres/vocabulary` lists them; their children rise one level: 49 genres from level 3 to 2, 5 from level 4 to 3, no level 4 left; the subfamily labels are kept); `Others` is removed from the parents of synthpop (parents Pop and new wave, level 2, not level 1) and disco (parent Funk / Soul only, level 2; italo disco, euro disco and the other disco kin follow it out of Others). Others keeps 82 genres (44 direct members). The legacy `/genres/graph` still has only the 11 families: the five forms map to Electronic, disco to Funk / Soul, synthpop to Pop. New `unparent` command in `scripts/genre-ops.py`. Side effect: an agreed `electronic` from MusicBrainz and Wikipedia no longer lets the song page apply techno, trance, house, hi-nrg and the like (they are no longer genres of the group Others). Not deployed.

## 2026-10-20 (hardcore renamed hardcore techno, built, deployed 2026-10-20)

- Data and server: the genre `hardcore` (Electronic, UK hardcore) is renamed `hardcore techno` in the genre data files and as the parent of happy hardcore, hard house, hard trance, hardstyle and the others (owner decision AX1); `hardcore` is a new `sound` modifier (defined, not attached, task-0065); no synonym. `app.migrate_renames` rewrites stored `genre:hardcore` tags to `genre:hardcore techno` once at start for every user (backup `backups-genre-rename/`, marker, idempotent, TAG-110, [deploy.md](tech/deploy.md#genre-rename)); the Discogs style "Hardcore" maps to `hardcore techno`. The Wikipedia lookup no longer suggests a bare `hardcore` (The Prodigy - Voodoo People loses that suggestion).

## 2026-10-20 (genre model written down, docs only, deployed 2026-10-20)

- Docs: ADR-0038 and a rewritten [genre-terminology.md](tech/genre-terminology.md) record the owner's genre model: genres versus groups (Electronic and EDM are groups), four axes (Form, Medium, Purpose, Tradition) plus personal Mood, modifiers as qualifiers of Form, the place, modifier and ambiguous-name tests, and the open decisions AN to AY; no data or code change.

## 2026-10-20 (Genres graph: wider rings, steadier selection)

- Web: Genres graph and Radial: a genre with several families (disco, euro disco, eurodance) is painted with the plain family colours in every wedge (Funk / Soul orange, Electronic teal, Pop pink) instead of the Electronic wedge taking the subfamily shade, which turned purple for the Eurodance & disco subfamily (`fillDisc` shades single-family discs only); dragging a child never moves its parent (`PARENT_REACTION` = 0). WEB-114, GEN-075, WEB-125. Deployed.
- Web: Genres graph: dragging a parent circle no longer gravitates toward its children, and every drawn descendant follows it with a 300 ms delay (`CHILD_FOLLOW_MS`, time constant a third of that) and ends at exactly the position relative to the parent it had before the move, toward or away (replaces the stretch-only leash; links to followers are muted meanwhile and the followers ride along until the simulation sleeps); dragging a child moves it freely and never moves its parent. Headless on the real vocabulary (disco, euro disco): relative offset error 0.00 px after a 120 px move toward and a 200 px move away. GEN-075 (7a), WEB-125. Deployed.
- Web: in Genres graph drill-down the children of an opened genre sit on a ring three times as wide (`RING_SCALE` = 3 in `genre-force-fan.js`: first ring, count-based radius, cap and the spacing of concentric rings, and the link rest length is the ring radius), so Rock's children are several circle radii from Rock and their labels no longer pile up on it; selecting a genre with several parents (house, then eurodance) no longer makes it jerk: newly drawn extra parents are placed on a small arc just outside the ring of its children with their rest positions there, and the camera stays put when the revealed set still fits the view (headless, real vocabulary: under 5 px in the first 300 ms). GEN-075 (2) and (6a), WEB-125. Deployed.

## 2026-10-19 (equal genre parents)

- Server and data: all parents of a genre are equal (owner decisions BY1, BX, CB2, [ADR-0037](tech/decisions.md), [task-0055](tasks/task-0055.md), GEN-070 to GEN-072): a genre belongs to every family it descends from and a song counts in each; the level is the shortest path depth; the depth cap of three levels is gone (sanity bound 12). `GET /genres/vocabulary` keeps `family`, `parents` and `level` and adds `families`; the graph nodes gain `families`; the `family` override is removed. The suggestion rule uses every parent and family.
- Data: disco is in Funk / Soul and Electronic and so are its children, post-disco is under disco; psytrance is under trance (its branch reaches level 4); eurodance has house, euro disco and Pop; darkwave has Electronic, post-punk and new wave; synthpop has Electronic, Pop and new wave; the genres industrial and ethereal are removed (stored tags dropped at startup; the alias "ethereal wave" is gone, so that tag is dropped too). Not applied: the larger nesting proposal (CA1, BV).
- Data: `electro house` is now a child of `house` alone, with `electro` as a `related` link, so it no longer belongs to the Hip Hop family (owner decision CT1); electroclash still reaches Hip Hop through electro.
- Data: `darkwave` now has only `post-punk` and `new wave` as parents (owner decision DB2): it is in Rock only (level 3), loses the Electronic subfamily "Synth & wave", and its child `coldwave` is Rock only too; Electronic 146 members, Rock 123 ([genre-levels.md](tech/genre-levels.md#equal-parents-and-the-edits-of-2026-10-19-adr-0037-task-0055)).
- Web: the Outline lists a genre under each parent, the family chips and counts count a song in each family, a genre of several families is a pie of equal segments in the family colours on the Radial and the Graph, the Radial draws deeper levels on further rings, and the Electronic family circle is not drawn on the map (its chip and Outline row still work).
- Fix: `GET /genres/vocabulary` `genres[].parents` listed the old Discogs/extras parents (psytrance under Electronic, electro house under Electronic, darkwave without post-punk and new wave, psybient without ambient) while `levels[name].parents` was right; the rows now carry the same full parent set as `levels` (`genre_vocab.build` takes the tree's parents); the Genres map graph draws every parent link as an equal `parent` link (the shallowest or alphabetical parent no longer becomes the only strong one, the rest weak `related`).
- Web: Genres graph children follow their parent: dragging a circle carries its whole subtree with a delay of about 300 ms (`CHILD_FOLLOW_MS`, exponential smoothing, deeper levels up to 400 ms; visible circles follow, hidden descendants' rest positions shift too, so nothing snaps back), revealed children start on a small ring around the parent with zero velocity and a gentle reheat (0.25), parent-child springs are stiffer (strength 1.4 / sqrt(min degree), floor 0.15 on the cooling factor) and velocity loss is 0.34 instead of 0.4; related links stay weak, reduced motion moves the subtree at once without lag. In drill-down a click on empty canvas collapses everything to the top level (GEN-075, WEB-125).
- Web: in Genres graph drill-down a click on a closed genre that is not a top-level circle, at level 2 as well as deeper (the families and the hidden Electronic root's genres stay top level and never hide anything; a genre with a drawn parent such as eurodance under Pop qualifies) hides its siblings with the branches they had open and keeps only the genre, its children and the full chains of all its parents (the parents are drawn closed; clicking one opens it as usual); clicking the selected genre again brings everything back as it was before the click; the same applies to a pick from the Outline, search or Now playing; top-level circles keep the browsing behaviour (GEN-075, WEB-125, `clickDrill` in `genre-drill.js`).
- Web: Genres graph dragging is now a one-way delayed leash instead of a rigid subtree move: a child follows its dragged parent only when the link is stretched beyond its rest length (eased, `CHILD_FOLLOW_MS` = 300, chained down the descendants, per parent link for several parents), moving a parent toward its child or a child toward its parent moves the other one not at all (slack tree links have no spring, charge or collision between a parent and child while one is held), dragging a child away moves the parent only a few pixels (`PARENT_REACTION` = 0.02 instead of 0.5), drag warmth 0.1 instead of 0.3 (`genre-force-view.js`, `genre-sim.js`, GEN-075 (7a), WEB-125). Not tested.
- Web: equal parents in the Genres graph code: a genre keeps all its parent links (no single last parent): dragging a circle also carries a genre when any of its parents is in the dragged subtree (unless another parent is pinned outside it), a selected genre highlights the chains of all its parents, new circles appear around the first parent already drawn, and "Now playing" falls back to the nearest drawn ancestor over all parents (`genre-force-view.js`, `genres-rework.js`).
- Web: selecting a genre on the Genres map highlights every ancestor along every parent path (for example spacesynth > italo disco > disco > Funk / Soul, a hidden Electronic root ends the chain at the topmost drawn ancestor): ancestor circles at full strength with labels, the chain edges in the selected-edge style, both fading gently with distance (GEN-043; `genre-force-view.js`).
- Web: the Genres map (Graph and Radial) shows no hover hint any more (no floating card with name, counts and status; owner request 2026-10-20, GEN-059); hover keeps the pointer cursor and the ring on the circle, the details stay in the click infobox and the Outline.
- Web: a click on a node of the Genres map (Graph and Radial), Enter in the search and the Outline no longer show the genre infobox (owner request 2026-10-20, "when click - don't show genre info box"): the click only highlights the node and its ancestor chain (and drills down on the Graph); the infobox, its "Show N songs in Library" button, the Sources icon row (`genre-sources-ui.js`) and their CSS are removed, and the existing page tests no longer expect them (GEN-014, WEB-087, WEB-109, WEB-111 withdrawn).
- Web: Genres graph parent links are one-way (owner request 2026-10-20): a child follows its parent as before, but a parent feels the child only when the child is pulled beyond the link's rest length, and then at half the former reaction (`parentReaction: 0.5` in `genre-sim.js`, set in `genre-force-view.js`); moving a child inside the rest length leaves the parent alone; related and association links stay symmetric.
- Web: Genres graph drill-down: opening a genre creates its children anew around it on every open (also after a collapse and reopen), spread evenly on a ring (angles evenly spaced; the fan opens away from the drawn parent over about 300 degrees, the full circle without one; more than 14 children use several concentric rings in proportion to their circumference), and the ring positions replace their layout rest positions, so the children no longer bunch on one side or sit far from the parent; children already drawn through another parent keep their place (`genre-force-view.js`, GEN-075, WEB-125).
- Data: `house` gets a `related` link to `disco` (owner decision DF1: house grew out of disco; an association, not a parent, so parents, families and counts do not change).

## 2026-10-19 (faster cold start, deployed 2026-10-20)

- Web: the radial view of the hidden Electronic family shows "Electronic" as the title at the centre (the circle stays hidden; GEN-072, deployed 2026-10-20).
- Web: the BPM badge is a rounded rectangle (`--radius-xs`, 4px) instead of a pill; the owner asked for about 3px, which needs a new radius token in the Design System.
- Web: the Library BPM badge moved out of the Genres cell to the right end of the subtitle line, after the in-queue icon and right-aligned under the song length; the Genres cell is plain text again and the reserved empty slot is gone; the badge shows at all widths (LIB-019, LIB-161, LIB-175, deployed 2026-10-20).
- Web: Library rows lose the "..." menu, the source logo and the right-hand cell (all duplicated by the song card). Each row ends its title line with one glyph-only azure state button left of the length: Download to the server (no audio), Add to queue (shown on row hover or focus), or the playlist glyph for a queued song (always visible, click removes); the progress ring replaces it during a download; no toast, the glyph changes. This reverses the passive in-queue icon of LIB-161. The only thing reachable solely through the old menu was "Play now", which the cover button still does (LIB-180, deployed 2026-10-20).

- Web: `/auth/me`, `/me` and `/library` start together and the Library is fetched once instead of twice (WEB-116); Settings, Genres map, Playlists, Song stats, Music theory and the visualizer load only when needed (WEB-117, WEB-118); the page tells the browser to fetch the whole startup module graph at once with generated `modulepreload` hints (WEB-119). Local cold start (60 ms latency, 5 Mbit/s): 173 to 110 JS files, DOMContentLoaded 7.7 s to 4.6 s.
- Web: two-tier loading, no build step: tier 1 (preloaded with `modulepreload`, 56 modules, 210 KB) is only what paints the Library list and the shell; the song card, Add and Edit with the song form, share dialog and links, tag picker, filter sheet and Drafts view load on a click with an inline "Loading..." note (WEB-120); once the first rows and their first 12 covers are in, the other 123 modules are warmed in the background 6 at a time when idle, never on data saver or 2g, and paused while a click is loading a module (tier 2, WEB-121, generated from the import graph); the first 12 covers load eagerly with high priority (WEB-122). Local cold start (60 ms, 5 Mbit/s): 110 to 58 JS requests before the first row, DOMContentLoaded 4.6 s to 2.5 s, first library row 6.0 s to about 3.7 s; all lazy modules cached after about 9 s.

## 2026-10-19 (Genre suggestion rule, deployed 2026-10-20)

- Server and web: genres from the lookups are applied only when independent sources agree, the rest are suggestions (decision EH1, ADR-0035, TAG-099 to TAG-108, API-186, ADD-181). Two groups out of MusicBrainz, Wikipedia song page, Wikipedia artist page, Discogs and the link's own tags must name a genre (a child counts for its ancestor); the Wikipedia song page alone also applies a genre that is not ambiguous and shares a family with an applied one; at most 5 are applied, a non-family ancestor is dropped beside its child. `POST /preview`, `/preview/stream`, `/metadata` and `/lookup/wikipedia` share one function (`metadata.resolve`), `tags` holds only the applied genres and the new `genre_suggestions` lists the rest with source badges; the Add and Edit forms show them as a "Suggested genres" row (click adds, x dismisses) and the old "From the original (not applied)" line is part of it. The artist infobox is no longer promoted to the song's own genres. Stored songs are not changed. Deployed 2026-10-20.

## 2026-10-19 (Genre terminology and parents lists, deployed 2026-10-20)

- Docs: new [tech/genre-terminology.md](tech/genre-terminology.md) (glossary, how "genre" is used in Wikipedia and catalogues, the genre by form, medium and purpose axes plus the subjective mood axis, open facets decision) and ADR-0034. Deployed 2026-10-20.
- Web: the Library search box has a clear (x) button on its right edge, shown while it has text; click or Escape clears the box and the filter and keeps focus (LIB-160). Deployed 2026-10-20.
- Web: the Library remembers the last used tab (Songs or Drafts) across reloads and navigation (localStorage key `electrophone.libraryView`, LIB-159); the Drafts view lost its "Clear all drafts" button and confirmation (per-draft Delete with undo stays; the API route `DELETE /drafts` is unchanged). Deployed 2026-10-20.
- Server data, docs and tests: the Rock family is drafted (owner decision DR1, GEN-067): 91 of the 111 Rock genres are placed under reviewed parents from Wikipedia lead sentences (42 level 2, 69 level 3, no level 4; krautrock and post-rock under experimental rock, hardcore punk and extreme metal as extra parents, folk metal, folk punk, rap metal and electronic rock marked as fusion genres, 11 genres gained `related`), 20 genres with an unsure review stay at Rock and provisional. Deployed 2026-10-20.
- Data (review only, not loaded): `server/data/genre_hierarchy_evidence.json` lists the Wikidata and Wikipedia parents of every genre with a Wikipedia match (472 of 549 with Wikidata parents, 208 with a lead-sentence parent), and `scripts/hierarchy-diff.mjs` compares them with `parents[]` per family. Deployed 2026-10-20.
- Server: every genre and family now records which of Discogs, MusicBrainz, Wikipedia and Apple Music has it (`server/data/genre_sources.json`, 449 MusicBrainz, 463 Wikipedia, 138 Apple Music of 540; ambiguous candidates in `genre_sources_review.json`, built by `scripts/build-genre-sources.mjs`); `GET /genres/vocabulary` rows gain `sources` and `source_links` (Wikipedia, Apple Music, Spotify, Discogs, MusicBrainz links built server side, ADR-0033, GEN-066). Deployed 2026-10-20.
- Server, web and docs: every node of the genre vocabulary is a genre and level 1 is a family (ADR-0030, GEN-057, GEN-058); the word "style" is gone from the UI, the docs, the data keys and the code except where it quotes the Discogs API. `genre_styles.json` is now `genre_discogs.json` (`families`, `genres`), and a genre has `parents` (a list: the first is the primary parent, the rest are cross-links) instead of `parent`, `also_under` and `also_in_genres`; the tree, the counts, the cross-links and the Genres map are unchanged (checked against a snapshot taken before the change). `GET /genres/vocabulary` now serves `level` family or genre, `parents`, `family` and `family_synonyms`; `GET /genres/graph` nodes carry `anchor` (and still `style` for the macOS app). Deployed 2026-10-20.
- Server and web: genre `spacesynth` is now level 3 under synthwave with italo disco as a cross-link, and the new genre `space disco` (a disco subgenre, approved, not in Discogs) sits at level 3 under disco in Electronic; Electronic now has 50 level 2 and 95 level 3 genres. Deployed 2026-10-20.
- Server: the flat `genres` row of `spacesynth` (from `genre_extras.json`) now lists synthwave first and italo disco second like its `levels` entry; new tests (`test_genre_payload_agrees.py`, `web/js/genre-payload.test.mjs`, fixture `server/tests/fixtures/genre_payload_levels.json`) fail when the served payload and `genre_levels.json` disagree. Deployed 2026-10-20.
- Web: the Genres map hover tooltip no longer prints the text "null" for the parts a node does not have (regression test added). Deployed 2026-10-20.
- Web: the Genres map opens as an Obsidian-style force-directed graph of all about 530 genres (library genres emphasised, family chips and a Library only toggle as filters, drag, pan, zoom, hover highlight, double-click zoom, smooth cooling physics; GEN-060, WEB-108) with a "Graph | Radial" switch that keeps the previous radial map; the force simulation now moves the lower-degree end of a link more (it was reversed); a genre with parents in several families is drawn in a split colour of all its families (up to 3, both views, legend line added); every parent link is one identical solid line and the infobox lists "Parents" without primary/extra wording; fusion genres (`fusion: true`) show under their second family only with the new "Fusion genres" switch (default off, GEN-063); the genre infobox is now its own panel next to the outline (WEB-109) with an empty source-icons slot; Radial stays one family at a time and opens on the selected node's family when switched to (GEN-062); a "Related" switch draws faint dotted lines for the new `related` data (GEN-066); the infobox lost its Children, Parent chain, Level, Families and In Discogs rows and gets a Sources row of icon links from the vocabulary `source_links` (WEB-111); split-colour genres in Radial now use their family colours (disco is Funk / Soul + Electronic; the umbrella hue had leaked in); genre names stay lowercase as the vocabulary gives them and songs listed only in the bare `genres` field now count on the map (GEN-065); the status is plain text above two one-line, horizontally scrolling button rows (WEB-110); Electronic genres are shaded by subfamily (up to ten shades of the family colour, pairwise at least 0.1 apart in OKLab, computed from the `subfamily` and `subfamilies` payload data, a Subfamily chip row filter, legend section and infobox row; WEB-114); the Graph now starts from a crossing-minimising layout (sector seed plus time-sliced annealing: 560 crossings of the plain force layout down to 154 with Fusion on, 405 down to 115 with it off, measured with the Rock hierarchy) and keeps it by a rest-position spring, with "Reset layout", an owner-only "Export layout" (clipboard JSON, download fallback) and an optional `web/data/genre-layout.json` with "Use saved layout" (WEB-112, WEB-113); hovering a circle only shows the tooltip and a ring, selection and neighbour highlight happen only on click (also in Radial, which already worked this way). Deployed 2026-10-20.
- Server and data: 22 genres whose parent link was only an association now carry it as `related` instead of `parents` (new optional list on a genre in `genre_levels.json`, served in `levels[genre].related`, validated; no effect on the tree, counts or colours; gospel moves to Folk, World, & Country, trap and acid jazz lose `fusion`); genre display names are lowercase; the genre `soviet wave` is now `sovietwave` (stored tags are migrated at startup, with the usual backup). Deployed 2026-10-20.
- Server and data: Electronic genres carry an optional `subfamily` (eight subfamilies declared: House, Techno, Trance, Breaks & bass, Synth & wave, Experimental industrial & noise, Ambient & downtempo, Eurodance & disco; declared in `genre_levels.json`; children inherit; payload `levels[genre].subfamily` and `subfamilies`, validated, GEN-068); the new genre `edm` takes electronic dance music, dance music, club music, electronic dance and dance from the Electronic family; `eurobeat` is now a synonym of `eurodance` (stored tags are rewritten at startup with the usual backup). Deployed 2026-10-20.
- Server and data: nine reviewed Rock genres added as extras (hardcore punk, experimental rock, extreme metal, noise rock, roots rock, neo-psychedelia, glam metal, college rock, death-doom; nothing moved under them yet) and electro-funk / electrofunk as synonyms of electro. Deployed 2026-10-20.
- Server: 23 Discogs genres that sit in two families now list the second family as an extra parent in `genre_levels.json` (24 multi-family genres with disco), and 12 blends (blues rock, folk rock, country rock, funk metal, jazz funk, soul jazz, acid jazz, latin jazz, hip house, trip hop, synthpop, trap) carry the new optional `fusion: true` flag, served in `GET /genres/vocabulary` `levels`; levels, families and counts are unchanged (GEN-061). The map toggle that uses the flag is separate. Deployed 2026-10-20.
- Web: the Drafts button "Add properly" is now "Add to Library" (label, aria-label, docs). Deployed 2026-10-20.
- Web: the Songs | Drafts switch in the Library was unstyled (plain browser buttons, the count as tiny text) because `drafts.css` was never linked from `index.html`; it is now linked, the buttons are 44 px tall and the count is a badge; new test `css-linked.test.mjs` fails when a stylesheet is not linked. Deployed 2026-10-20.

## 2026-10-18 (Library draft list)

- Server and web: the Library has a "Songs | Drafts N" switch. In Drafts the owner and every signed-in user paste "Artist - Title" lines and links (one per line, Ctrl or Cmd+Enter), get a quick YouTube or SoundCloud lookup (title, channel, length, upload year, thumbnail; two lookups at a time, stops on a 429) and keep them as a per-user list in `users/<uid>/drafts.json` (not in `library.json`; guests get 401; up to 500 drafts, 100 lines and 300 characters per line per paste). Rows show an "In library" mark, Refresh, Edit, Delete with undo and "Add to Library" (opens the Add dialog with the link, removes the draft after saving); New routes `GET/POST/DELETE /drafts`, `PATCH /drafts/{id}`, `POST /drafts/{id}/refresh`, `DELETE /drafts/{id}`. ## 2026-10-17 (data model v2, built, not deployed)

- Server: songs are stored as `mainArtists`, `featuredArtists`, `releaseYear`, `albums`, `version {type, name, artists, releaseYear, originalArtists}`, `rawTitle` and `sources {platform, url}` (DATA-156); decade is computed at runtime, `lang:` tags are gone, `country:` tags moved to a per-user Artist table (`GET /artists`, `PATCH /artists/{name}`). `artist`, `remixer`, `remix_year`, `year`, `album` and `tags` stay derived in every response, so iOS is unchanged; old and flat names are still accepted on input for one release. New one-time migration `app.migrate_model2` (dry run, derived-field check that aborts on any difference, backup, review file) replaces `migrate_credits`; runbook in deploy.md.
- Web: reads and sends the v2 names, decade chips and filters computed from `releaseYear`.
- Server and web: the decade (Years filter, chips, `decade:` in the tags view) follows the displayed year, the version year when there is one (DATA-161).
- Server: genres are stored bare in `genres[]` (no `genre:` prefix); `tags[]` is kept only for other leftovers; responses derive the prefixed `tags` view for iOS; `PATCH`/`POST` accept `genres` or legacy prefixed `tags`; the web edit dialog sends `genres`.
- Server: changing country tags on a song with several main artists is refused with 422 (DATA-160); the edit dialog shows the message inline.

## 2026-10-16 (git sync: per-save local commit, timer pushes, built, not deployed)

- Server, web and ops: a save writes atomically and commits locally, with no pull and no push in the request (NFR-111); `audioplayer-gitsync.timer` (60 s) runs `python -m app.gitsync`, which only pushes repos with unpushed commits; a rejected push stops that repo and is `error`, never merged (ADR-0026, STO-054 to STO-056). `/health` `sync` is now an object with `ok|pending|stale|error`, counts, ages and a timer heartbeat for the owner and only `ok|degraded` for everyone else (API-137); Settings shows a mark on the sync card and a dot on the nav item while `stale` or `error`. `GIT_SYNC_MODE=request` keeps the old path for one release. Units in `deploy/systemd/`, check with `scripts/check-gitsync.sh`; root must install the timer before the state returns to `ok` ([deploy.md](tech/deploy.md#git-sync-timer)). Fixes: cover downloads are staged outside the repo and written atomically under the git lock, a failed save removes only the files its own transaction created, and `scripts/check-domain.sh` accepts the object form of the guest `/health` (`degraded` is a warning).

## 2026-10-16 (documentation: data model v2 and the versions concept)

- Docs: ADR-0025 records data model v2 (final names, the version block with `type`, `originalArtists` for cover and mashup only, the Artist table, runtime decade, DDEX adopted only for the display-artist role names and `version.type`, full DDEX and a Work/chain model deferred); new concept note [concept-songs-versions-and-credits](product/concept-songs-versions-and-credits.md); [ddex-terminology](tech/ddex-terminology.md) and task-0043 added. ADR-0026 plans per-save local commits with a systemd timer that only pushes, plus a Settings warning mark when pushes lag (amends ADR-0005; task-0021, NFR-111 to NFR-114, WEB-094, deploy.md and architecture.md updated; see the git sync entry above). The changelog sections below are marked deployed or not deployed according to production at `c813d92`.

## 2026-10-16 (structured credits and versions, deployed)
- Fix: a legacy value that fails validation is kept in `raw.rejected` instead of being dropped; a version marker is no longer hidden by a title word that merely contains it (Recovery, Alive); the deploy runbook warns against rolling code back across the credits migration.

- Server: songs now store `artists`, `featured`, `released`, an optional `version {kind, name, by, year, of}` and an immutable `raw`; `artist`, `remixer`, `year` and `remix_year` are derived in every response and still accepted in requests, so the iOS app is unchanged (DATA-127 to DATA-139, API-120 to API-135, IOS-072 to IOS-074, ADR-0024, [design](tech/metadata-model-design.md)).
- Server: an idempotent startup migration converts old songs without splitting any credit, with a backup in `backups-credits/` and a review file of songs worth a look; `/preview` adds `raw`, `suggested_credits` and `released_suggestion`; `released` no longer falls back to the upload date (DATA-126); a song with a version never gets a MusicBrainz or Discogs link.
- Web: the Add and Edit form has Artists, Featuring, First released, Version (kind, name, year, remixed / mixed by, cover of) and a Suggested split with Apply and Dismiss; the library, Now playing, share page, stats and the Media Session show one display line from the structure (ADD-111/112, LIB-134). Dry run on a copy of the live library: 201 songs, every derived artist, remixer, year and remix_year equal to the original, 63 songs listed for review. iOS needs no change; deployed.

## 2026-10-16 (web style overhaul, deployed)

- Design system: the Electrophone artifact (version 26) gains token families chart, stroke, state, motion, elevation, overlay, icon and control, `radius-xs`, `space-5`, `power-text`, two type styles, 15 web components (Icon, SearchField, SortControl, Toggle, Slider, Dialog, Sheet, Toast, Menu, TokenPill, Banner, StatCard, GenreMap, VisualizerChrome, SignIn) and a 45-icon Lucide and Simple Icons set; `docs/brand/tokens.json` is byte-identical to the artifact copy.
